Algorithm outline:
- Normalize a sum=1 (or n? Use sum=1 for objective M; score=2n*M). To avoid optimizer messing with huge, use sum=1; clip.
- Maintain current a, b=max, score.
- Adaptive temperature T: λ_k ∝ exp((b_k - max_b)/T). If T too small only top; too large average. We can try anneal. Or use top-K uniform: K dynamic e.g. 10% of coefficients with b≥τ.
- Update direction: r = cross_corr(a, λ).

Let's focus on improving local search. The previous local search may be weaker. We can design a more effective optimization using active weights λ and map a_i ∝ 1/√r_i. This directly targets all coefficients. Let's detail and maybe test concept mentally for some sequences.

Given a (sum S=1), b=conv(a,a). Choose λ over coefficients with b near max. For each i, r_i = ∑_k λ_k a_{k-i} (with valid j). Then update a_i^new ∝ a_i / r_i^γ (γ maybe 0.5, or step). If γ=1, smaller r increase more. This tends to equalize r_i, meaning ∑λ_k a_{k-i} constant; KKT for active weighted sum. If λ is distribution over max b, and r_i equal, weighted sum ∑λ b has stationary. Repeating with λ adaptively should reduce max. It is like minimizing a variational problem.

Algorithm can be:
1. Normalize a to sum 1 (or n). Use sum=1 to simplify score=2n*maxb.
2. Compute b. score=2n*max(b).
3. Build λ: Let active threshold maybe max_b - τ. Use softmax with temperature T = α * (max_b - mean_b) or based on quantile. Try multiple temperatures.
4. Compute r via convolution. r_i = sum_j a_j λ_{i+j}. If r_i zero, set to small.
5. Direction: log_new = log(a) - γ * log(r / mean_r?) Actually gradient descent in log domain for objective max? For weighted sum W=∑λ b, using multiplicative update a_i ← a_i / r_i^γ reduces W if γ between 0 and ? 6. Normalize sum to 1. Accept if score improves. If not, reduce step or try alternative λ.

```
def mix_score_coeffs(aa, bb, cr): # arrays length L
   A = bb - 2*cr + aa
   B = 2*(cr - aa)
   C = aa
   def score_at(t): return 2*n*np.max(A*t*t + B*t + C)
```

Let's design `local_search` loop:
```
def local_search(a, budget, seed, allow_lp=True):
  n=len(a); t0...
  a=normalize(a,1)
  b=conv(a); score=2n*max(b)
  best=a.copy(); best_score=score; best_b=b.copy()
  it=0; stagn=0
  last_lp=0
  while time<deadline:
     # compute b of current maybe if updated
     # Try AWU for e.g. up to 10 candidates per loop
     improved=False
     # generate λ selections: maybe 4 per iteration based on current b
     for lam in make_lambda(b, M, it):
        r=compute_r(a,lam)
        for theta in (0.25,0.5,1.0):
           cand = active_candidate(a,r,theta)
           cand_b=conv(cand); cand_score=...
           # line search?
           if cand_score <= best_score: update...
           if line_search improves...
           if improved: break
     if not improved:
        # maybe gradient step? perturbe
        # Optionally LP
        if allow_lp and n<=1200 and time since lp>lp_interval:
           for _ in range(2): h=lp_direction(a); line search...
        # smooth perturb if stagn large
```

Another strong method: Since the constraint set for fixed M is {a≥0 sum1, b(a)≤M}. We can try feasibility for decreasing M via interior point / projected gradient. Start M from current best. To reduce M, use multiplicative updates over all constraints: If b_k > M, decrease contributions? This is like minimizing sum exp(b_k/T). Active-weight temperature does that.

Let's consider exact KKT at optimum: There exist λ_k≥0, sum λ_k=1 such that active set b_k=M and for all i>0: r_i = const. The score=2n M. This can be solved by alternating:
- Given λ, find a stationary by a∝1/r iterates.
- Given a, set λ over active (or near-active) constraints.
This is exactly our AWU. We can implement this more rigorously:
  λ = top-K active with K maybe all near max. Then perform fixed-point iterations for a until r_i nearly constant (gradient entropy). Then evaluate b; update λ. This might converge to KKT.

LP objective and scaling:
```
def lp_direction(a, seed, max_var):
    n=len(a); L=2*n-1
    a_use = np.maximum(a, 0.0) + floor
    A = build_A(a_use)
    c = - (1 + 1e-3*rng.random(n)) # for linprog minimize -c
    res = linprog(c, A_ub=A, b_ub=np.ones(L), bounds=[(0,max_var)]*n, method='highs', options={'time_limit': 30})
    if res.success: h=res.x; clip; normalize; return d
```

```
def local_search(a, budget_s, seed=0, allow_lp=True, lp_max_n=2048):
    n = len(a)
    t0=time.monotonic(); deadline=t0+max(0,budget_s)
    a=normalize(a,1.0)
    b=fast_convolution(a)
    score=2*n*np.max(b)
    best=a.copy(); best_score=score; best_b=b.copy()
    rng=np.random.default_rng(seed)
    stagn=0; last_lp=t0-1000; it=0
    while time.monotonic() < deadline:
        it+=1
        # ensure finite
        # compute λ candidates and gradient candidates
        improved=False
        M=float(np.max(b))
        # Selection of lambda: top fractions and temps; number depends time
        # e.g. for idx, lam in enumerate(make_lambda_list(b, M, it, rng, max_candidates=6)):
        for lam in make_lambda_list(b, M, it, rng):
            r = compute_r(a, lam)
            r_mean = float(np.mean(r))
            r_scale = max(1e-300, float(np.max(np.abs(r))))
            for eta in (0.05, 0.1, 0.2, 0.35):
                normr = r / r_scale
                delta = -eta * (normr - float(np.mean(normr)))
                # optionally add random small noise
                if rng.random() < 0.1:
                    delta += rng.normal(0, 0.01, n)
                delta = np.clip(delta, -3, 3)
                cand = a * np.exp(delta)
                cand = normalize(cand,1.0)
                cb = fast_convolution(cand)
                cs = 2*n*float(np.max(cb))
                # line search between a and cand? maybe if cs < best_score*1.1
                best_t, cand_ls, score_ls = line_search(a,b,cand,cb,n)
                if score_ls < score: # improved from current
                    a=cand_ls; b=fast? we can compute from mix using t?
                    score=score_ls
                if score_ls < best_score:
                    best=cand_ls; best_score=score_ls; improved=True
                if improved: break
            if improved: break
        if improved:
            b=fast_convolution(a) # ensure
            stagn=0
        else:
            # Try LP if allowed and time
            if allow_lp and n <= lp_max_n and (time.monotonic()-last_lp) > lp_interval(n):
                last_lp=time.monotonic()
                # up to 2 directions
                for lp_i in range(2):
                    d = lp_direction(a, seed+it*10+lp_i, max_var=max(100.0, 10.0*n))
                    if d is not None:
                        db=fast_convolution(d); cr=fast_convolve(a,d)
                        t,dnew,s = line_search(a,b,d,db,n, cr=cr)
                        if s < best_score:
                            best=dnew; best_score=s; a=dnew; b=fast_convolution(a); improved=True; break
                if improved: stagn=0
            if not improved:
                stagn += 1
                if stagn > 30:
                    # random smooth perturbation of best
                    cand = smooth_perturb(best, rng, strength=0.1 + 0.3*rng.random())
                    cb=fast_convolution(cand); cs=...
                    if cs < best_score:
                        best=cand; best_score=cs; a=cand; b=cb
                    else:
                        a = 0.9*best + 0.1*smooth_perturb(best,...) normalized? maybe
                    # also restart from best
                    a = best.copy()
                    b = fast_convolution(a)
                    score = best_score
                    stagn = 0
    return best,best_score
```

```
def line_search(a, b_a, d, b_d, n, cr=None, current_score=None):
   if cr is None: cr=fast_convolve(a,d)
   A = b_d - 2*cr + b_a
   B = 2*(cr - b_a)
   C = b_a
   # grid Ts = np.linspace(0,1,41)
   def evals(T):
      vals = C[None,:] + B[None,:]*T[:,None] + A[None,:]*T[:,None]**2
      scores = 2*n*np.max(vals, axis=1)
   T = np.linspace(0,1,57)
   sc = evals(T)
   idx=int(np.argmin(sc))
   # refine around T[idx] with small width
   for _ in range(2):
       width=max(0.02, T[1]-T[0])
       lo=max(0,T[idx]-width); hi=min(1,T[idx]+width)
       T2=np.linspace(lo,hi,33); sc2=evals(T2); idx2=argmin
       T[idx]=T2[idx2]
       if abs(...)<1e-5 break
   t=float(T[idx]); d_new = (1-t)*a + t*d; normalize? Since both sum1, linear combo sum1 unless t. No need normalize but do for safety. score=sc[...]
   return t,d_new,float(score)
```

Need `fast_convolve(x,y)` for arbitrary arrays. Use rfft. Need handle real. 
```
def fft_convolve(x,y):
   n=len(x); m=len(y); L=n+m-1; N=1 << (L-1).bit_length()
   fx=np.fft.rfft(x,N); fy=np.fft.rfft(y,N)
   out=np.fft.irfft(fx*fy,N)[:L]
   return np.ascontiguousarray(out)
```
For self, use y=x and maybe fa*fa. To avoid conjugate? fine.

`make_lambda_list` deterministic plus random:
```
def make_lambda_list(b, M, it, rng, max_count=6):
   L = len(b)
   lams=[]
   # topK fractions: 1, max(1,L//100), L//20, L//5, L
   for frac in (0.0,0.01,0.05,0.1,0.3):
      K = int(L*frac)
      if K < 1: K=1
      if K > L: K=L
      if K==L: lam=np.full(L,1/L)
      else:
         lam=np.zeros(L); lam[np.argpartition(b,-K)[-K:]] = 1.0/K; lams.append(lam)
   # softmax
   spread = M - float(np.mean(b))
   if spread <= 0: spread=M+1e-300
   for T_frac in (0.05,0.15,0.4):
      T=T_frac*spread
      # use b/M maybe and floor M - 5*spread
      floor = max(0.0, M - 5*spread)
      w=np.exp((b-floor)/T)
      if not finite or sum<=0: w=np.ones(L); w[np.argmax(b)]=10
      lams.append(w/w.sum())
   # if it even, add uniform? maybe
   # Shuffle or take up to max_count
   if len(lams)>max_count: select first? Use rng permutation? No lambdas.
   return lams[:max_count]
```
Computing argpartition for each K repeated could be costly but L small. Could cache top indices? Not.
Maybe top-only λ with K=1 often stuck; but included.
To avoid too many, max_count=4 in expensive loop. Could select based on it round-robin.

Build A efficiently:

```
def build_convolution_matrix(a):
   n=len(a); L=2*n-1
   A=np.empty((L,n), dtype=np.float64)
   # zero? all entries assigned exactly once? Let's check rows length either k+1 or L-k; sum lengths = n(n+1)/2 + (n-1)n/2 = n^2, every row? Actually total entries not n^2? L*n = (2n-1)n ~2n^2, many zeros. If using empty and assigning only nonzero, need fill zeros. So initialize zeros and assign.
   A = np.zeros((L,n), dtype=np.float64)
   for k in range(n):
      A[k, :k+1] = a[k::-1]
   for k in range(n, L):
      start = k - (n-1)
      length = L - k
      A[k, start:start+length] = a[n-1 : n-1-length : -1]
   return A
```
Check row lengths: first k=n-1 length n, assign all. second k=n start1 length n-1, assign A[n,1:]=a[n-1:0:-1]; A[n,0]=0. But row n should have valid i=1..n-1? For k=n, i+j=n, i can 1..n-1? yes A[n,0] zero. Good. For k=n+1 start2 length n-2, A[n+1,2:]=a[n-1:1:-1]; zeros else. Good. For k=2n-2 start=n-1 length1, A[2n-2,n-1]=a[n-1]; endpoint. Good.
Total assignments n(n-1) plus? row lengths sum: rows 0..n-1 sum n(n+1)/2; rows n..2n-2 sum (n-1)n/2; total n^2. Matrix nonzeros ~n^2, zeros ~n^2. Good.

LP objective c: minimize -w·h, c=-w. Ensure finite.
```
def lp_direction(a, seed, max_var, time_limit=30):
   if linprog is None: return None
   n=len(a); floor=1e-6
   a_use = np.maximum(a, 0.0) + floor
   A=build_convolution_matrix(a_use)
   one=np.ones(2*n-1)
   rng=np.random.default_rng(seed)
   w = np.ones(n) + 0.1*rng.standard_normal(n)
   w=np.clip(w,0.05,10.0)
   c = -w
   bnds=(0.0, max_var)
   try:
       options={'time_limit': int(max(1, min(60, time_limit)))}
       res=linprog(c, A_ub=A, b_ub=one, bounds=bnds, method='highs', options=options)
       if not res.success:
           res=linprog(c, A_ub=A, b_ub=one, bounds=bnds, method='highs')
   except Exception:
       try: res=linprog(c, A_ub=A, b_ub=one, bounds=bnds, method='highs')
       except Exception: return None
   if not res.success: return None
   h=np.asarray(res.x).ravel()
   h=np.where(np.isfinite(h), h, 0.0)
   h=np.clip(h,0.0,max_var)
   s=h.sum()
   if s<=1e-15: return None
   return h/s
```
Note: If `max_var` large and n large, bounds tuple repeated: `[(0.0, max_var)] * n` creates list of same tuple okay. Could use `bounds=(0,max_var)`? linprog accepts single tuple? It accepts iterable; single tuple interpreted as (min,max) for all? I think `bounds=(0, max_var)` applies to all? Safer `bounds=[(0.0, max_var)]*n`.
Memory: A plus one. For n=2048, A 8M 64MB, c etc. Good maybe <1GB.
Could use `A` as 32-bit for memory? linprog may require float64. Keep float64.

`smooth_perturb`: multiplicative low-freq and local. Keep nonnegative. Use sum1 normalize.
```
def smooth_perturb(a, rng, strength):
   n=len(a); a=a.copy()
   t=np.linspace(0,1,n)
   z=np.zeros(n)
   for m in range(1,5):
       amp=strength/(1+m)
       ph=rng.uniform(0,2*np.pi)
       z += amp*np.cos(2*np.pi*m*t + ph)
   a *= np.exp(z)
   # local random bumps
   for _ in range(3):
       width=max(1, n//100)
       start=int(rng.integers(0, max(1,n-width+1)))
       w=np.linspace(0,1,width); bump=np.sin(np.pi*w)**2
       sign=rng.choice([-1,1])? No rng.choice okay.
       a[start:start+width] *= np.exp(sign*strength*bump)
   return normalize(a,1.0)
```
Need choose sign without lambda: use `if rng.random()<0.5: sign=-1 else 1`.

```
def line_search(a,b_a,d,b_d,n, cr=None):
   if cr is None: cr=fast_convolve(a,d)
   A = b_d - 2*cr + b_a
   B = 2*(cr - b_a)
   C = b_a
   # define nested? No closures. Need helper functions top-level? We can make `evaluate_mix_scores(A,B,C,T,n)` top-level.
   T = np.linspace(0,1,65)
   sc = eval_mix_scores(A,B,C,T,n)
   # flatten indices: np.argsort(sc)[:3]
   best_t=0; best_sc=np.inf; best_new=None
   for idx in order:
       center=T[idx]; width=2*(T[1]-T[0])
       for _ in range(2):
          lo=max(0, center-width); hi=min(1, center+width)
          if hi<=lo: break
          T2=np.linspace(lo,hi,25); sc2=eval_mix_scores(A,B,C,T2,n)
          j=int(np.argmin(sc2)); center=float(T2[j]); width*=0.4
       # final new at center
       new = (1-center)*a + center*d; new=normalize(new,1.0)
       scf,bnew=score_from_sequence(new,n)
       if scf<best_sc: ...
   return best_new,best_b,best_sc
```

`normalize_to_sum`:
```
def normalize_to_sum(a, target=1.0):
    a=np.asarray(a,dtype=np.float64)
    if a.size==0: return np.ones(1)*target? but n>0.
    a=np.where(np.isfinite(a), a, 0.0)
    a=np.maximum(a,0.0)
    a=np.minimum(a,1000.0/target? Actually sum target 1, max 1000 no issue. For general target, clip max 1000.
    s=float(a.sum())
    if s <= 1e-300:
       a=np.full(a.size, target/a.size)
       return a
    a = a * (target/s)
    a=np.where(np.isfinite(a), a, 0.0)
    # if clipping caused sum change? For target1 no clip.
    return a
```
Since target=1, max values maybe <1? Could a normalized have max>1000? If one element all mass, max=1, okay. So no clip needed. But if upsample from huge values? We normalize to 1, max≤1. Good.

`score_and_conv`:
```
def score_and_conv(a, n):
    a=np.asarray(a)
    if a.size != n: n=a.size
    b=fast_conv_self(a)
    s=float(a.sum())
    if s < 1e-15: return inf,b
    # score independent of sum; use b/s^2
    maxb=float(np.max(b)) if b.size else inf
    return float(2*n*maxb/(s*s)), b
```
We maintain sum1 but for starts maybe. Use this in add_start.

`fast_conv_self` with N power of two:
```
def fast_conv_self(a):
    n=len(a); L=2*n-1
    if n==0: return np.empty(0)
    N=1 << (L-1).bit_length()
    fa=np.fft.rfft(a,N)
    c=np.fft.irfft(fa*fa,N)[:L]
    c[np.abs(c)<1e-14?]=0? Use np.maximum(c,0,out=c) to remove negative roundoff. But if true tiny positive maybe okay. np.maximum(c,0).
    return np.ascontiguousarray(c)
```
For nonnegative, coefficients nonnegative. Use maximum.

`fft_convolve`:
Similar for x,y; result can have small negative? Convolution of nonnegative, maximum 0. Use np.maximum.

Could cache rfft of a to speed? Since a changes each iteration. Could precompute fa = rfft(a,N) and use for self and cross? But cross with different y length N same maybe. For self N=nextpow2(2n-1), for compute_r cross with revλ length 2n-1, N=nextpow2(3n-2) maybe larger. Not same always? For n=1024, self N=4096, cross N=4096. For n=2048, self N=4096, cross N=8192. So not. But we could still recompute.

Need handle n=1: L=1, conv length1, start0. `return np.array([float(conv[0])])`. For n>1: `return np.ascontiguousarray(conv[start : n-2 : -1].copy())`. `make_lambda_list` details and performance:
```
def make_lambda_list(b, M, it, rng, max_count=6):
    L=len(b)
    out=[]
    # top1
    lam=np.zeros(L); lam[int(np.argmax(b))]=1; out.append(lam)
    # fractions
    fractions=(0.02,0.08,0.2,0.6)
    for frac in fractions:
       K=int(np.ceil(frac*L)); K=max(1,min(L,K))
       if K==L: lam=np.full(L,1.0/L)
       else:
          idx=np.argpartition(b, L-K)[-K:]
          lam=np.zeros(L); lam[idx]=1.0/K
       out.append(lam)
    # softmax
    meanb=float(np.mean(b))
    spread=M-meanb
    if spread<=1e-300: spread=M if M>0 else 1.0
    for tf in (0.05,0.15,0.4):
       T=tf*spread
       floor=M-5*spread
       if floor<0: floor=0
       # to avoid underflow: max log maybe 0 (b-floor)/T <=5
       w=np.exp(np.clip((b-floor)/T, -50, 50))
       s=w.sum()
       if s<=0: w=np.ones(L); w[int(np.argmax(b))]=10
       out.append(w/s)
    # maybe uniform
    out.append(np.full(L,1.0/L))
    # Select a rotating subset to avoid same each loop
    if len(out) <= max_count: return out
    # Use it to choose start: select indices from out by ordering but rotate
    # top-level no lambda; create list of selected
    selection=[]
    base=it % len(out)
    for ii in range(max_count):
       selection.append(out[(base+ii)%len(out)])
    return selection
```
This returns many; local_search loops maybe max 3. Instead of returning all, local_search can iterate over list and break early. Use max_count=4 maybe.
But repeatedly computing argpartition for 4 fractions expensive? L=1023, argpartition O(L) ~1k, fine. For many iterations, 4*1k*1000=4M, okay. Could cache top partitions? Not.
Potential issue: softmax floor = M-5spread; if spread small and floor=M, only max expt0? uniform? fine.

`local_search` detailed code:
```
def local_search(a, budget_s, seed, allow_lp=True, lp_max_n=1500):
    n=int(len(a))
    a=normalize_to_sum(a,1.0)
    t0=time.monotonic(); deadline=t0+max(0.0,float(budget_s))
    score,b=score_and_conv(a,n)
    best=a.copy(); best_score=score; best_b=b.copy()
    rng=np.random.default_rng(seed)
    it=0; stagn=0; eta=0.15; accept=1e-12
    lp_interval=max(5.0, 120.0/float(n))
    last_lp=t0
    while time.monotonic() < deadline:
       if not np.all(np.isfinite(best)): ...
       it+=1
       improved=False
       M=float(np.max(b)) if b.size else score/(2*n)
       lams=make_lambda_list(b,M,it,rng,max_count=3)
       for lam in lams:
          r=compute_r(a,lam)
          if not finite: continue
          rmax=float(np.max(r))
          if rmax<=0: continue
          rn=r/rmax
          meanrn=float(np.mean(rn))
          # Try a few eta values
          etas=(eta, max(0.02,eta*0.5), min(0.8,eta*1.8))
          best_cand=None; best_cand_score=score; best_cand_b=None
          for e in etas:
             delta=-e*(rn - meanrn)
             # add noise occasionally
             if rng.random()<0.05:
                delta=delta + rng.normal(0,0.02,n)
             delta=np.clip(delta,-3,3)
             cand=np.exp(np.log(np.maximum(a,1e-300)) + delta)  # if a zero, exp(log eps) small
             cand=normalize_to_sum(cand,1.0)
             cs,cb=score_and_conv(cand,n)
             if cs < best_cand_score:
                 best_cand_score=cs; best_cand=cand; best_cand_b=cb
          if best_cand is not None and best_cand_score < score*1.05 + 1e-12:
             # line search
             t, new, nb, ns = line_search(a,b,best_cand,best_cand_b,n)
             if ns < best_cand_score:
                best_cand_score=ns; best_cand=new; best_cand_b=nb
          if best_cand is not None and best_cand_score < score - 1e-14:
              a=best_cand; b=best_cand_b; score=best_cand_score; improved=True; eta=min(0.8, eta*1.05)
              if score < best_score - 1e-14:
                  best=a.copy(); best_score=score; best_b=b.copy()
              break
          else:
              eta=max(0.02, eta*0.8)
       if not improved:
          # try LP if allowed
          if allow_lp and linprog is not None and n <= lp_max_n:
             lp_wait = 2.0 if n <= 600 else (5.0 if n <=1200 else 10.0)
             if time.monotonic() - last_lp > lp_wait:
                last_lp=time.monotonic()
                # try up to 3 LP directions? If time left enough
                max_dirs = 1 if n>1000 else 3
                for lp_idx in range(max_dirs):
                   d=lp_direction(a, int(seed + 1000*it + lp_idx), max_var=max(100.0, 20.0*n), time_left=max(1.0, deadline - time.monotonic() - 30.0))
                   if d is None: continue
                   db,? We need score_and_conv(d) gives b_d and score.
                   sc_d, bd = score_and_conv(d,n)
                   cr=fft_convolve(a,d)
                   t, new, nb, ns = line_search(a,b,d,bd,n, cr=cr)
                   if ns < score - 1e-14:
                       a=new; b=nb; score=ns; improved=True
                       if ns < best_score - 1e-14: best=a.copy(); best_score=ns; break
                   if time_left < 30: break
       if improved:
          stagn=0
          if score < 0.0? no
       else:
          stagn += 1
          if stagn > 20:
              # restart around best with perturbation
              strength=0.1 + 0.4*rng.random()
              pert=smooth_perturb(best, rng, strength)
              ps, pb = score_and_conv(pert,n)
              if ps < best_score:
                 best=pert; best_score=ps
              # choose a as blend of best and pert maybe
              a=0.8*best + 0.2*pert; a=normalize_to_sum(a,1.0)
              score,b=score_and_conv(a,n)
              if score > best_score*1.05:
                 a=best.copy(); b=best_b.copy(); score=best_score
              eta=0.1+0.2*rng.random()
              stagn=0
       # safety: if time almost out, break
    return best, best_score
```

`line_search`:
Need score normalization. It returns new b maybe unnormalized? `score_and_conv(new,n)` normalizes by sum^2. Since new sum1, consistent.
```
def line_search(a,b_a,d,b_d,n,cr=None):
    if cr is None: cr=fft_convolve(a,d)
    A = b_d - 2*cr + b_a
    B = 2*(cr - b_a)
    C = b_a
    T = np.linspace(0.0,1.0,65)
    sc = eval_mix_scores(A,B,C,T,n)
    order = np.argsort(sc)[:3]
    best_t=0.0; best_new=a; best_b=b_a.copy(); best_sc=score_and_conv? use 2n*max(C)
    grid_w=1.0/64
    for idx in order:
       center=float(T[idx]); width=2*grid_w
       for _ in range(2):
          lo=max(0.0, center-width); hi=min(1.0, center+width)
          if hi-lo < 1e-6: break
          T2=np.linspace(lo,hi,25); sc2=eval_mix_scores(A,B,C,T2,n)
          j=int(np.argmin(sc2)); center=float(T2[j]); width *= 0.4
       new = (1.0-center)*a + center*d
       new = normalize_to_sum(new,1.0)
       scf, bnew = score_and_conv(new,n)
       if scf < best_sc:
          best_sc=scf; best_t=center; best_new=new; best_b=bnew
    return best_t,best_new,best_b,best_sc
```

```
def eval_mix_scores(A,B,C,T,n):
   Tcol=T.reshape(-1,1)
   vals=C.reshape(1,-1) + B.reshape(1,-1)*Tcol + A.reshape(1,-1)*Tcol*Tcol
   return float(2.0*n*float(np.max(vals, axis=1)))? Need array. Return np.asarray(2*n*np.max(vals,axis=1), dtype=float)
```

```
m=np.max(vals, axis=1); m[~finite]=inf.
```

`run_level` with DE:
```
def run_level(n,budget,parents,seed,allow_lp=True):
   if budget < 20:
      seq = upsample best parent or ones
      return seq, score
   rng=np.random.default_rng(seed)
   starts=[]
   # parents
   for p in parents:
      add_start(starts, upsample_sequence(p,n),n)
   # deterministic shapes: add multiple
   add_start(starts, np.ones(n),n)
   for p in (0.2,0.5,1.0,2.0,3.0,4.0):
      for q in (0.0,0.5,1.0,2.0): add beta
   # reversed by p/q covers? add q/p? beta t^p(1-t)^q covers p>q up, etc.
   for frac in (0.1,0.25,0.4,0.5,0.6,0.75,0.9):
      for ratio in (0.2,0.5,1.5,3,6): add two block
   for _ in range(8): add sinusoid, blocks
   # DE starts if n<=1024 and budget>180:
   if de_available and n <= 1024 and budget > 240:
      de_starts = get_de_starts(n, 120, seed) # maybe fixed time? DE doesn't know exact time; use maxiter small
      for seq in de_starts: add
   # sort starts
   starts.sort(key=_pair_score)
   # select unique? Could avoid duplicate identical scores by comparing scores.
   # choose top k
   k=1
   if budget > 300: k=2
   if budget > 600 and n <= 1024: k=3
   k=min(k,len(starts))
   # allocate times
   if k==1: times=[budget*0.9]
   elif k==2: [0.65,0.25]
   else: [0.55,0.3,0.15]
   best_seq=None; best_score=inf
   for i in range(k):
      if time.monotonic() > deadline: break
      start_seq = starts[i][1]
      time_this = remain * fractions[i]
      seq, sc = local_search(start_seq, time_this, seed+i, allow_lp=allow_lp and n<=lp_max_n maybe)
      if sc < best_score: update
   # if no improvements, return best start or parent
```

DE objective: Need normalize. Sequence from knots: K values. To avoid all zero, add floor. 
```
def de_objective(x, n, K):
    x=np.asarray(x,dtype=np.float64)
    if x.size != K: return np.inf
    if not np.all(np.isfinite(x)): return np.inf
    x=np.clip(x,0.0,4.0)
    t_old=np.linspace(0,1,K) if K>1 else [0]
    t_new=np.linspace(0,1,n)
    y=np.interp(t_new, t_old, x)
    y=np.maximum(y,1e-12)
    # maybe small noise? deterministic
    y=normalize_to_sum(y,1.0)
    s,_=score_and_conv(y,n)
    return s
```
No seed needed. DE `args=(n,K)`.
`get_de_starts`:
```
def get_de_starts(n, budget, seed):
    out=[]
    if differential_evolution is None or budget < 60: return out
    if n <= 768: K=16; maxiter=60; popsize=12
    else: K=24; maxiter=30; popsize=12
    bounds=[(0.0,4.0)] for K -> top-level cannot create with lambda? List comprehension okay no lambda? `bounds = [(0.0, 4.0)] * K` fine.
    for run in range(2 if n<=768 else 1):
       try:
          res=differential_evolution(de_objective, bounds, args=(n,K), maxiter=maxiter, popsize=popsize, tol=1e-6, init='latinhypercube', seed=seed+run, polish=False, workers=1)
          if res.x is not None:
             seq=knots_to_seq(res.x,n,K)
             sc,_=score_and_conv(seq,n)
             out.append((sc,seq))
       except Exception: pass
    return out
```

```
def make_sinusoid(n,rng):
   t=np.linspace(0,1,n)
   f=int(rng.integers(1,5))
   ph=rng.uniform(0,2π)
   amp=float(rng.uniform(0.2,0.8))
   y=1.0 + amp*np.cos(2π*f*t+ph)
   f2=int(rng.integers(1,6)); ph2=...
   y += 0.5*amp*np.sin(2π*f2*t+ph2)
   y=np.maximum(y,0.05)
   y *= np.exp(rng.normal(0,0.05,n))
   return normalize(y,1)
```

```
def knots_to_seq(x,n,K):
    x=np.asarray(x)
    if K==1: y=np.full(n, float(x[0]) if x[0]>0 else 1.0)
    else:
       t_old=np.linspace(0.0,1.0,K)
       t_new=np.linspace(0.0,1.0,n)
       y=np.interp(t_new, t_old, x)
    y=np.maximum(y,1e-12)
    return normalize_to_sum(y,1.0)
```

`fast_conv_self`:
```
def fast_conv_self(a):
   a=np.asarray(a,dtype=np.float64)
   n=a.size
   if n==0: return np.empty(0,dtype=np.float64)
   L=2*n-1
   N=1 << (L-1).bit_length()
   fa=np.fft.rfft(a,N)
   fa=fa*fa
   c=np.fft.irfft(fa,N)[:L]
   c=np.maximum(c,0.0)
   return c
```
If n=1, L=1, N=1? `(L-1).bit_length()` for 0 ->0, 1<<0=1. rfft length1 okay.

`fft_convolve`:
```
def fft_convolve(x,y):
   x=np.asarray(x,dtype=np.float64); y=...
   L=x.size+y.size-1
   if L<=0: return empty
   N=1 << (L-1).bit_length()
   fx=np.fft.rfft(x,N); fy=np.fft.rfft(y,N)
   out=np.fft.irfft(fx*fy,N)[:L]
   out=np.maximum(out,0.0) # but if x,y nonnegative. For cross a,d nonnegative. For line_search cr nonnegative. okay.
   return out
```
If we ever use for other? only nonnegative. Good.

`score_and_conv`:
```
def score_and_conv(a,n):
   a=np.asarray(a,dtype=np.float64)
   n=int(a.size)
   s=float(a.sum())
   if s <= 1e-300:
      return float('inf'), np.zeros(2*n-1)
   b=fast_conv_self(a)
   if b.size==0: return inf, b
   maxb=float(np.max(b))
   sc=float(2.0*n*maxb/(s*s))
   if not np.isfinite(sc): sc=float('inf')
   return sc,b
```
We often pass n but use a.size.

`compute_r`:
```
def compute_r(a,lam):
   n=len(a)
   if n==0: return np.empty(0)
   if n==1:
      return np.array([float(a[0]*lam[0])])
   rev=lam[::-1]
   conv=fft_convolve(a, rev)
   start=2*n-2
   # ensure conv long enough
   if conv.size <= start:
      # fallback zeros
      return np.zeros(n)
   seg=conv[start : n-2 : -1]
   if seg.size != n:
      # fallback: direct? Not needed.
      seg = np.zeros(n)
   return np.ascontiguousarray(seg)
```
Check slice for n=2 gives 2 elements; n-2=0. For n=1 handled. If conv size exactly start+1 for n? For n=2 conv len4 start2 okay. Good.

`eval_mix_scores`:
```
def eval_mix_scores(A,B,C,T,n):
   Tn=np.asarray(T,dtype=np.float64).reshape(-1,1)
   vals=C.reshape(1,-1) + B.reshape(1,-1)*Tn + A.reshape(1,-1)*(Tn*Tn)
   m=np.max(vals, axis=1)
   m=np.where(np.isfinite(m), m, np.inf)
   return (2.0*float(n))*m
```
Return array.

`line_search`:
```
def line_search(a,b_a,d,b_d,n,cr=None):
   a=np.asarray(a); d=np.asarray(d)
   if cr is None: cr=fft_convolve(a,d)
   A=b_d - 2.0*cr + b_a
   B=2.0*(cr - b_a)
   C=b_a.copy()
   base_sc = float(2.0*n*np.max(C))
   best_t=0.0; best_seq=a.copy(); best_b=C.copy(); best_sc=base_sc
   T=np.linspace(0.0,1.0,65)
   sc=eval_mix_scores(A,B,C,T,n)
   order=np.argsort(sc)[:3]
   grid_w=1.0/64.0
   for idx in order:
      center=float(T[idx]); width=2*grid_w
      for _ in range(2):
         lo=max(0.0, center-width); hi=min(1.0, center+width)
         if hi - lo < 1e-7: break
         T2=np.linspace(lo,hi,25)
         sc2=eval_mix_scores(A,B,C,T2,n)
         j=int(np.argmin(sc2))
         center=float(T2[j])
         width *= 0.4
      new = (1.0-center)*a + center*d
      new=normalize_to_sum(new,1.0)
      scf,bnew=score_and_conv(new,n)
      if np.isfinite(scf) and scf < best_sc:
         best_sc=scf; best_t=center; best_seq=new; best_b=bnew
   return best_t,best_seq,best_b,best_sc
```
Could skip recompute for t=0? It's okay.
Potential issue: `A,B,C` from FFT may have small negative; evaluation could yield negative max? But max nonnegative due b_a. No.

`make_two_block`:
```
def make_two_block(n, frac, ratio):
   if n<2: return normalize_to_sum(np.ones(n, dtype=float),1.0)
   m=int(round(frac*float(n)))
   m=max(1,min(n-1,m))
   denom=m*float(ratio)+(n-m)
   if denom<=0: return normalize_to_sum(np.ones(n),1.0)

   a=np.empty(n); a[:m]=ratio/denom; a[m:]=1/denom; return normalize_to_sum(a,1.0)
```

`make_beta_shape`:
```
def make_beta_shape(n,p,q,rng,noise=0.08):
   if n<2: ...
   t=(np.arange(n)+0.5)/float(n)
   # if t endpoints 0; p/q 0 -> 0^0? Python `0**0` =1, okay. But t maybe 0? (0.5/n)>0. 1-t >0.
   y = np.power(t, p) * np.power(1.0-t, q)
   if noise:
      y = y * np.exp(rng.normal(0.0, float(noise), n))
   y = np.maximum(y, 1e-12)
   return normalize_to_sum(y,1.0)
```
`rng.normal` returns array. Good.

`make_sinusoid`:
```
def make_sinusoid(n,rng):
   t=np.linspace(0,1,n)
   y=np.ones(n)
   f=int(rng.integers(1,5)); ph=float(rng.uniform(0,2*np.pi))
   amp=float(rng.uniform(0.2,0.8)); y += amp*np.cos(2*np.pi*f*t+ph)
   f2=int(rng.integers(1,6)); ph2=float(rng.uniform(0,2*np.pi))
   amp2=float(rng.uniform(0.1,0.5)); y += amp2*np.sin(2*np.pi*f2*t+ph2)
   y = np.maximum(y, 0.02)
   y *= np.exp(rng.normal(0,0.04,n))
   return normalize_to_sum(y,1.0)
```

`make_block_shape`:
```
def make_block_shape(n,rng,num_blocks=None):
   if n<4: return normalize_to_sum(np.ones(n),1.0)
   if num_blocks is None: num_blocks=int(rng.integers(4,15))
   num_blocks=int(max(2,min(num_blocks,n//2)))
   edges=np.linspace(0,n,num_blocks+1)
   edges=np.round(edges).astype(int)
   edges[0]=0; edges[-1]=n
   # handle duplicates
   lens=np.diff(edges)
   if np.any(lens<=0):
      edges=np.unique(np.linspace(0,n,num_blocks+1).astype(int))
      if edges[0]!=0: edges=np.concatenate(([0],edges))
      if edges[-1]!=n: edges=np.concatenate((edges,[n]))
      if len(edges)<2: return normalize_to_sum(np.ones(n),1.0)
      lens=np.diff(edges)
   vals=np.exp(rng.uniform(-1.0,1.0,len(lens)))
   if rng.random()<0.25:
      vals[int(rng.integers(0,len(vals)))] = 0.0
   a=np.repeat(vals, lens)
   if a.size != n: # pad/truncate
      tmp=np.zeros(n); tmp[:min(n,a.size)] = a[:min(n,a.size)]; a=tmp
   return normalize_to_sum(a,1.0)
```
Could produce all zeros if one zero? Normalize handles if sum zero by uniform.

`random_shape`:
```
def random_shape(n,rng):
   r=float(rng.random())
   if r < 0.34:
       p=float(rng.integers(0,5)) / 2.0  # 0,0.5,1,1.5,2? int 0-4 ->0..2
       q=float(rng.integers(0,4)) / 2.0
       return make_beta_shape(n,p,q,rng,noise=0.08)
   elif r < 0.6:
       return make_sinusoid(n,rng)
   else:
       return make_block_shape(n,rng)
```

`upsample_sequence`:
```
def upsample_sequence(seq,n):
   seq=np.asarray(seq,dtype=np.float64)
   m=seq.size
   n=int(n)
   if m==0: return normalize_to_sum(np.ones(n),1.0)
   if m==n: return normalize_to_sum(seq,1.0)
   xold=np.linspace(0,1,m) if m>1 else np.array([0.5])
   xnew=np.linspace(0,1,n)
   y=np.interp(xnew, xold, seq)
   y=np.maximum(y,0.0)
   return normalize_to_sum(y,1.0)
```
If m=1, xold [0.5] but np.interp with single point? `np.interp` requires xp length >0 and increasing; one point okay? It returns y[0] constant? I think yes? Actually np.interp with xp length 1 works? It may. To be safe:

```
if m==1: return normalize_to_sum(np.full(n, float(seq[0])),1.0)
```

```
def generate_starts(n, parents, rng, use_de=False, de_budget=0):
   starts=[]
   for p in parents:
      if p is not None and p.size>0:
         add_start(starts, upsample_sequence(p,n), n)
   add_start(starts, np.ones(n), n)
   # beta shapes
   pvals=[0.0,0.5,1.0,1.5,2.0,3.0]
   qvals=[0.0,0.5,1.0,1.5,2.0]
   for p in pvals:
      for q in qvals:
         add_start(starts, make_beta_shape(n,p,q,rng,noise=0.02), n)
   # if too many? 6*5=30
   # two block
   for frac in (0.1,0.25,0.4,0.5,0.6,0.75,0.9):
      for ratio in (0.2,0.5,1.5,3.0,6.0):
         add_start(starts, make_two_block(n,frac,ratio), n)
   # random shapes 8
   for _ in range(8): add_start(starts, random_shape(n,rng), n)
   if use_de:
      de_starts=get_de_starts(n, de_budget, int(rng.integers(0,100000)))
      for sc,seq in de_starts: add_start(starts, seq,n)
   if not starts: add_start(starts, np.ones(n),n)
   starts.sort(key=_pair_score)
   return starts
```

`build_convolution_matrix`:
```
def build_convolution_matrix(a):
   n=len(a); L=2*n-1
   A=np.zeros((L,n), dtype=np.float64)
   for k in range(n):
      A[k, :k+1] = a[k::-1]
   for k in range(n, L):
      start = k - (n-1)
      length = L - k
      A[k, start:start+length] = a[n-1 : n-1-length : -1]
   return A
```
Test for row k=n-1 first loop: A[n-1,:]=a[n-1::-1] good. For row n loop: start1,length n-1, A[n,1:]=a[n-1:0:-1]. n=2: L3, first row0 [a0,0], row1 [a1,a0]; second loop k=2 start1 length1 A[2,1]=a[0]? Wait n=2, endpoint k=2: i=1,j=1 term a1^2, not a0. Our second loop formula for k=2: start = 2 -1 =1, length=3-2=1, a[n-1 : n-1-length : -1] = a[1 : 0 : -1] = [a1]. Good. But row2 should A[1]=a1. Good. I confused with k=2n-2 for n>2? For n=3, k=4 endpoint: start=2,length1, a[2:1:-1]=[a2]. Good. For n=4, k=6 endpoint: start=3,length1, a[3:2:-1]=[a3]. Good. For k=n upper: n=4,k=4,start1,length3, a[3:0:-1]=[a3,a2,a1], row4 should i=1..3 terms a3,a2,a1. Good.
For left rows k=0..n-1: row k i=0..k terms a_k...a0. Good.

`lp_direction`:
```
def lp_direction(a, seed, max_var, time_left=30.0):
   if linprog is None: return None
   n=len(a)
   if n < 2: return None
   floor=1e-5
   a_use=np.asarray(a,dtype=np.float64).copy()
   a_use=np.clip(a_use,0.0,1000.0)
   a_use = a_use + floor
   A=build_convolution_matrix(a_use)
   one=np.ones(2*n-1, dtype=np.float64)
   rng=np.random.default_rng(seed)
   w=np.ones(n, dtype=np.float64)
   w += 0.2 * rng.standard_normal(n)
   # bias towards small a? maybe
   med=float(np.median(a))
   if med > 0:
      w += 0.5 * (med / (a + floor))
   w=np.clip(w,0.05,50.0)
   c=-w
   bnds=[(0.0, float(max_var))] * n
   res=None
   try:
       tl=int(max(1, min(60, max(1.0, time_left))))
       res=linprog(c, A_ub=A, b_ub=one, bounds=bnds, method='highs', options={'time_limit': tl})
   except Exception:
       try:
           res=linprog(c, A_ub=A, b_ub=one, bounds=bnds, method='highs')
       except Exception:
           return None
   h=None
   if res is not None:
      x=getattr(res,'x',None)
      if x is not None:
         h=np.asarray(x, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
         h=np.where(np.isfinite(h), h, 0.0)
         h=np.clip(h,0.0,float(max_var))
   if h is None or float(h.sum()) <= 1e-15:
      # maybe use status? if not success no x
      if res is not None and getattr(res,'success',False):
         ... (x handled)
      return None
   return h/float(h.sum())
```
The bias `0.5*med/(a+floor)` if a=0 huge, clipped to50. This encourages mass at low coordinates. Could be beneficial but maybe biases. Keep moderate? Might cause directions spiky. Random enough. Could remove to keep pure. Let's use only random? Maybe `w = 1 + 0.2 rand + 0.2*(1 - normalized a?)`? Let's keep simple `w = 1 + 0.2 normal`. The LP will explore all vertices, no bias. If a has near-zero, column small, LP may choose it anyway due objective independent. Random enough. Use `w = np.clip(w,0.05,10)`. Good.
Potential large max_var and objective random: Solution h can hit cap at many variables; if all cap, direction uniform, not a vertex. Maybe okay. Use max_var=10*n? For uniform n=1024, cap10240 > n so no cap. Good.

```
def run_level(n,budget_s,parents,seed,allow_lp=True):
   t0=time.monotonic(); deadline=t0+max(0.0,float(budget_s))
   if budget_s < 30:
      if parents: seq=upsample_sequence(parents[0],n)
      else: seq=np.ones(n)
      seq=normalize_to_sum(seq,1.0); sc,_=score_and_conv(seq,n); return seq,sc
   rng=np.random.default_rng(seed)
   use_de = (differential_evolution is not None) and (n <= 1024) and (budget_s > 200)
   de_budget = min(120.0, 0.15*budget_s)
   starts=generate_starts(n, parents, rng, use_de=use_de, de_budget=de_budget)
   # remove worst? keep top maybe 10 for time? Not needed.
   k=1
   if budget_s > 300: k=2
   if budget_s > 600 and n <= 1024: k=3
   k=min(k,len(starts))
   fractions = {1:(1.0,), 2:(0.65,0.3), 3:(0.55,0.3,0.15)}[k]
   best_seq=None; best_score=np.inf
   for i in range(k):
      remain=deadline - time.monotonic()
      if k>1 and i<k-1:
          if remain < 30: break
          time_this = max(15.0, remain*fractions[i])
      else:
          time_this = max(0.0, remain - 5.0)
      if time_this <= 5.0: break
      start_seq=starts[i][1]
      try:
         seq,sc=local_search(start_seq, time_this, int(seed*10+i), allow_lp=allow_lp)
      except Exception:
         seq=start_seq; sc,_=score_and_conv(start_seq,n)
      if np.isfinite(sc) and sc < best_score:
         best_seq=seq; best_score=sc
         print(f"[level {n}] start {i} score {sc:.7f}")
   if best_seq is None:
      best_seq=starts[0][1]; best_score=starts[0][0]
   return best_seq,best_score
```

`sanitize_for_return`:
```
def sanitize_for_return(seq):
   a=np.asarray(seq,dtype=np.float64)
   if a.size==0: return [1.0]
   a=np.where(np.isfinite(a), a, 0.0)
   a=np.clip(a,0.0,1000.0)
   s=float(a.sum())
   if s < 0.01:
       return [1.0]
   a = a * (1.0/s)  # sum1
   a=np.clip(a,0.0,1000.0)
   if float(a.sum()) < 0.01:
       return [1.0]
   # if huge length and memory? okay.
   return [float(x) for x in a]
```
If a sum1, ok. If a all clipped 0? fallback.

Pseudo full local_search:
```
def local_search(a, budget_s, seed, allow_lp=True):
    n=int(len(np.asarray(a)))
    a=normalize_to_sum(a,1.0)
    t0=time.monotonic(); deadline=t0+max(0.0,float(budget_s))
    if deadline - t0 < 5: return a, score...
    score,b=score_and_conv(a,n)
    best=a.copy(); best_score=score; best_b=b.copy()
    rng=np.random.default_rng(seed)
    it=0; stagn=0; eta=0.15
    allow_lp = allow_lp and (linprog is not None) and (n <= 1024)
    lp_wait = 3.0 if n <= 600 else 8.0
    last_lp=t0
    while time.monotonic() < deadline:
        rem=deadline-time.monotonic()
        if rem < 3: break
        it+=1
        improved=False
        M=float(np.max(b)) if b.size else 0.0
        lams=make_lambda_list(b,M,it,rng,max_count=3)
        for lam in lams:
            r=compute_r(a,lam)
            if r.size != n: continue
            rmax=float(np.max(r)) if r.size else 0.0
            if rmax <= 1e-300 or not np.isfinite(rmax): continue
            rn=r/rmax
            meanrn=float(np.mean(rn))
            delta=-eta*(rn-meanrn)
            # occasional noise
            if rng.random() < 0.05:
               delta=delta + rng.normal(0.0,0.02,n)
            delta=np.clip(delta,-4.0,4.0)
            cand=a*np.exp(delta)
            cand=np.maximum(cand,1e-18)
            cand=normalize_to_sum(cand,1.0)
            cs,cb=score_and_conv(cand,n)
            new=cand; newb=cb; ns=cs
            if np.isfinite(cs) and cs < score*1.05 + 1e-12:
                t_new, new, newb, ns = line_search(a,b,cand,cb,n)
            if np.isfinite(ns) and ns < score - 1e-14:
                a=new; b=newb; score=ns; improved=True
                if ns < best_score - 1e-14:
                    best=a.copy(); best_score=ns; best_b=b.copy()
                eta=min(0.8, eta*1.05)
                break
            else:
                if cs < score:
                    # improve current but line search didn't? accept? The cs<score would have ns=cs if no line search? If line search not used due threshold? If cs<score, threshold true, line search, ns <=? maybe line search returned t=0 score base maybe score. But if line search bug? Accept cs?
                    pass
                eta=max(0.03, eta*0.85)
        if not improved:
            if allow_lp and (time.monotonic() - last_lp) > lp_wait:
                last_lp=time.monotonic()
                max_dirs = 3 if n <= 600 else 1
                for d_idx in range(max_dirs):
                    rem2=deadline - time.monotonic() - 30.0
                    if rem2 < 30.0: break
                    d=lp_direction(a, int(seed + 100000 + it*100 + d_idx), max(100.0, 20.0*n), rem2)
                    if d is None: continue
                    sc_d, bd = score_and_conv(d,n)
                    if not np.isfinite(sc_d): continue
                    cr=fft_convolve(a,d)
                    t_new, new, newb, ns = line_search(a,b,d,bd,n,cr=cr)
                    if np.isfinite(ns) and ns < score - 1e-14:
                        a=new; b=newb; score=ns; improved=True
                        if ns < best_score - 1e-14:
                            best=a.copy(); best_score=ns; best_b=b.copy()
                        break
        if improved:
            stagn=0
        else:
            stagn+=1
            if stagn > 15:
                strength=0.1 + 0.5*rng.random()
                pert=smooth_perturb(best,rng,strength)
                ps,pb=score_and_conv(pert,n)
                if np.isfinite(ps) and ps < best_score:
                    best=pert; best_score=ps; best_b=pb
                # move current toward perturb
                mix = 0.25 + 0.5*rng.random()
                a = (1.0-mix)*best + mix*pert
                a = normalize_to_sum(a,1.0)
                score,b=score_and_conv(a,n)
                if score > best_score*1.05 + 1e-12:
                    a=best.copy(); b=best_b.copy(); score=best_score
                eta=0.1 + 0.3*rng.random()
                stagn=0
            if stagn > 60:
                # try a completely new shape blended with best
                shape=random_shape(n,rng)
                mix=0.35
                a=(1.0-mix)*best + mix*shape
                a=normalize_to_sum(a,1.0)
                score,b=score_and_conv(a,n)
                if score > best_score*1.1 + 1e-12:
                    a=best.copy(); b=best_b.copy(); score=best_score
                stagn=0
    return best, best_score
```

```
def smooth_perturb(a,rng,strength):
    n=len(a); a=np.asarray(a,dtype=np.float64).copy()
    if n > 2:
       t=np.linspace(0,1,n)
       z=np.zeros(n)
       for m in range(1,5):
          amp=strength/(1.0+0.5*m)
          ph=float(rng.uniform(0,2*np.pi))
          z += amp*np.cos(2*np.pi*m*t + ph)
       a *= np.exp(z)
    # local bumps
    for _ in range(2):
       width=max(1, min(n//50, n)) # if n small
       width=max(1,width)
       start=int(rng.integers(0, max(1, n - width + 1)))
       stop=min(n, start+width)
       seg=np.linspace(0,1,stop-start)
       bump=np.sin(np.pi*seg)**2
       if rng.random() < 0.5:
          a[stop_start?]=a[start:stop]*np.exp(-strength*bump)
       else:
          a[start:stop]=a[start:stop]*np.exp(strength*bump)
    a=np.maximum(a,1e-18)
    return normalize_to_sum(a,1.0)
```

Let's write `make_lambda_list` robust:
```
def make_lambda_list(b, M, it, rng, max_count=3):
   L=len(b)
   lams=[]
   if L<=0: return lams
   # top1
   lam=np.zeros(L); lam[int(np.argmax(b))]=1.0; lams.append(lam)
   # top fractions
   for frac in (0.03,0.15,0.5):
       K=int(np.ceil(frac*float(L)))
       K=max(1,min(L,K))
       if K>=L:
          lam=np.full(L,1.0/float(L))
       else:
          lam=np.zeros(L)
          idx=np.argpartition(b, L-K)[-K:]
          lam[idx]=1.0/float(K)
       lams.append(lam)
   # softmax
   meanb=float(np.mean(b))
   spread=M-meanb
   if spread <= 1e-300: spread = M if M > 0 else 1.0
   for Tfrac in (0.1,0.3):
       T=max(1e-300, Tfrac*spread)
       floor=M-5.0*spread
       if floor < 0: floor = 0.0
       exponent=(b - floor)/T
       exponent=np.clip(exponent, -50.0, 50.0)
       w=np.exp(exponent)
       s=w.sum()
       if s <= 1e-300:
          w=np.full(L,1.0); w[int(np.argmax(b))]=5.0
       lams.append(w/float(w.sum()))
   # uniform
   lams.append(np.full(L,1.0/float(L)))
   # rotate tail
   if len(lams) <= max_count: return lams
   base=it % len(lams)
   # We want keep top1/softmax maybe? Simpler select last max_count rotated from 2..end to avoid always top1? But top1 useful.
   selected=[lams[0]]
   start=1
   pool=lams[1:]
   if len(pool) <= max_count-1:
       selected.extend(pool)
   else:
       rot=it % len(pool)
       for ii in range(max_count-1):
           selected.append(pool[(rot+ii)%len(pool)])
   return selected
```

This direction is piecewise constant; line search in full space. Good. Could be powerful and cheap. Add top-level:
```
def block_average(a,m):
   n=len(a); m=int(m)
   if m<=1: return np.asarray([float(a.mean())])
   if m>=n: return np.asarray(a)
   edges=np.linspace(0,n,m+1).astype(int)
   sums=np.zeros(m); counts=np.diff(edges)
   for i in range(m): sums[i]=a[edges[i]:edges[i+1]].sum()
   # counts >0
   return sums/np.maximum(counts,1)
```
`lp_direction_coarse(a, seed, m, max_var, time_left)`:
```
ac=block_average(a,m)
# scale ac? LP constraints use ac coefficients as weights; we just need direction. To avoid unbounded, add floor.
h=lp_direction(ac, seed, max_var=max(10,m*10), time_left)
if h is None: return None
# expand to n using same block edges as block_average? Need return edges.
```
Need block edges. Could write helper `block_edges(n,m)`. Or within. Simpler: use `np.repeat(h, block_lengths)` where block_lengths from `np.diff(edges)`. Need edges. Write:
```
def make_block_edges(n,m):
   if m<=1: return np.array([0,n])
   if m>=n: return np.arange(n+1)
   edges=np.linspace(0,n,m+1).astype(int)
   # fix duplicates: ensure increasing
   edges[0]=0; edges[-1]=n
   j=1
   prev=0
   while j < len(edges)-1:
      if edges[j] <= prev:
         edges[j]=prev+1
         # if equals next? better use unique resolution
      prev=edges[j]; j+=1
   # If still duplicates at end, fallback
   if np.any(np.diff(edges) <=0):
```

```
def block_average(a,m):
   n=len(a); m=int(m)
   if m<=1: return np.array([float(a.mean())])
   if m>=n: return np.asarray(a,dtype=float).copy()
```

```
def block_average(a,m):
   n=len(a); m=int(m)
   if m <=1: return np.array([float(a.mean())])
   if m >= n: return np.asarray(a,dtype=np.float64).copy()

   edges=np.unique(edges)
```
